Practice grammar in context — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. REFERENCE
Course: Housekeeping and Commercial Cleaning English
Professional audience: Housekeeping attendants, commercial cleaners, custodial staff, and cleaning team members
Scope: All sites, rooms, quantities, guests, and incidents are fictional. This book teaches workplace English, not chemical handling, equipment operation, hazard control, legal advice, or specialist infection prevention. Follow actual training, product labels and safety data sheets, site access rules, privacy and found-property procedures, and emergency arrangements. No example authorizes unsafe access, use of an unidentified product, or release of an unchecked area.
Lesson 06: Counting linen and replenishing supplies
Original fictional case: The third-floor cart target is twelve bath towels and eight hand towels. Attendant Noor counts nine bath towels and eight hand towels. A laundry delivery is expected but its arrival and contents are not confirmed. Supply lead Ben needs an item-specific report and a request for three additional bath towels, not a statement that all linen is unavailable. No delivery has been received or counted. The target is a cart-stock requirement, not a claim about the number of occupied rooms or a reason to substitute one towel type for another.
Language workshop: Three bath towels short
Communication goal: Report linen counts by item, calculate the exact shortfall, and separate expected delivery from stock on hand.
Language guidance: Bath towels: target twelve, counted nine, short three. Hand towels: target eight, counted eight, no numerical shortfall. Keep item types separate even when giving a combined cart total.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): This is the third-floor linen cart. / The target is twelve bath towels. / I counted nine bath towels. / We are three bath towels short. / Hand towels are eight against a target of eight. / The shortage is not across all linen. / Please arrange three additional bath towels. / The laundry delivery is expected but not confirmed. / I have not added expected items to the current count. / Has the delivery actually arrived and been checked? / Do we know what the delivery contains? / Hand towels are not an assumed replacement for bath towels. / I will revise the count after the actual receipt and checks. / Three bath towels, not three carts or packs. / The bath-towel shortfall remains open. / Nine bath towels and eight hand towels are currently counted.
Vocabulary: linen: Textile items used in housekeeping, such as towels and bedding. / bath towel: Larger towel intended for drying the body after bathing. / hand towel: Smaller towel intended primarily for drying hands. / bath mat: Textile or other item used on the floor near bathing facilities. / pillowcase: Cover used over a pillow. / duvet cover: Removable fabric cover for a duvet. / flat sheet: Unfitted bed sheet without shaped elastic corners. / fitted sheet: Sheet designed to fit around a mattress. / linen cart: Mobile storage unit carrying linen supplies. / floor allocation: Supplies assigned to a particular building floor. / stock target: Quantity specified as the desired amount on hand. / par level: Planned stock level used for replenishment decisions. / physical count: Quantity actually counted at the location. / stock on hand: Supplies currently present, not merely expected. / shortfall: Amount by which the current count is below the target. / item type: Category of supply that must remain distinct in the count. / replenishment request: Request to restore the required stock. / laundry delivery: Transfer of linen from the laundry supply process. / expected delivery: Delivery anticipated but not yet confirmed as received. / confirmed receipt: Established arrival and acceptance under the relevant process. / delivery contents: Items and quantities actually included in a delivery. / count discrepancy: Difference between a recorded or expected amount and a checked count. / substitution: Replacement of one item type with another under the required approval. / stock update: Revised statement of actual inventory after a verified change.
Speaking task: Read the linen count, stressing three short rather than nine short. Say the bath-towel calculation and the separate hand-towel comparison aloud. Complete the new cart count without adding an expected delivery to stock on hand.
Partner: Noor: Room attendant / Ben: Supply lead
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: The second-floor cart target is ten bath towels and six hand towels. The count is seven bath towels and six hand towels. A delivery is expected but not confirmed.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Noor: I will update you after receipt and checking. Until then, the bath-towel count stays at nine.
Ben: That is when the stock update should change the recorded figures. We should preserve the earlier count rather than retroactively pretend the cart was already full.
Noor: My final read-back is third-floor cart: nine bath towels against twelve, eight hand towels against eight, with three bath towels still needed.
Ben: Agreed. The stock target remains twelve and eight respectively. The delivery is expected, not confirmed, and the request is for three additional bath towels.
END REFERENCE
Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

07Responding to a quality concernStreaks beside office 8+

Your goal

Acknowledge a reported result, identify the exact surface, and arrange a check without inventing the cause or outcome.

This lesson in the book, page 58 ↗

A Read the situation

Streaks beside office 8

Client Dana reports streaks on the interior glass panel beside office 8, which was cleaned earlier today. Cleaner Malik has not yet checked the reported area with the client. Dana is available to point out the panel. Lead Rosa can arrange a check and appropriate rework through the site process. The cause is unknown, and no rework or final inspection is complete. Malik needs to acknowledge the concern, locate it precisely, and explain the next step without denying the report, blaming someone, or promising a result and time that have not been verified.

Dana: Client contact / Malik: Cleaner

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. Which surface is reported?

Answer and explanation

C. Interior glass panel beside office 8

The brief identifies the interior panel and its location beside office 8.

2. What is known about the cause?

Answer and explanation

D. It is unknown

The report identifies a result but does not establish its cause.

3. What can Dana do now?

Answer and explanation

D. Point out the panel

Dana is available to identify the relevant panel, while checking and rework remain pending.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Streaks are the reported result. They do not prove the wrong product, permanent damage, or who caused the problem. Acknowledge the report and identify the panel before discussing what the actual check establishes.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
quality concern
Report that a result may not meet the required standard.acknowledge a quality concern
streak
Visible line or mark remaining across a surface.report streaks on glass
smear
Spread or blurred mark on a surface.describe a smear
residue
Material remaining after an activity or substance has been removed.identify residue
haze
Cloudy appearance that reduces surface clarity.describe a haze
scratch
Mark or damage caused by abrasion or contact.distinguish a scratch from residue
interior glass
Glass surface on the inside of a building or assembly.identify interior glass
glass panel
Defined sheet or section of glass.locate the glass panel
partition
Structure dividing areas within a space.identify the partition
frame
Surrounding support or border of a panel.distinguish the frame from glass
edge
Boundary of a surface or object.identify the affected edge
finish
Visible or specified final condition of a surface.assess the finish
inspection
Examination against the relevant requirements.arrange an inspection
rework
Additional work to correct a result that does not meet requirements.arrange appropriate rework
verification check
Check that the specified result has actually been achieved.complete a verification check
reported result
Outcome described by the person raising the concern.acknowledge the reported result
fluorescent marker
Tracer used to check whether a marked spot was physically cleaned; removal does not prove disinfection.assess fluorescent-marker removal
cause analysis
Investigation of why a result occurred.separate cause analysis from acknowledgement
service standard
Required level or condition of the provided service.check the service standard
client feedback
Information from the client about the service received.receive client feedback
follow-up arrangement
Agreed method or responsibility for the next action.confirm a follow-up arrangement
ATP
Adenosine triphosphate; a cleaning-test signal for organic material, not a direct count of specific pathogens.interpret an ATP result
resolution
Established outcome that addresses the issue through the actual process.confirm resolution
update commitment
Promise to provide information about progress, not a guaranteed result.make an update commitment

Cleaned earlier is a past activity, not proof of the present finish. Rework arranged, rework completed, and result verified are different stages. Report the real stage without promising a perfect finish or an unchecked completion time.

C Notice the language

Acknowledge the result before explaining the process

Saying we already cleaned it may sound like a refusal to listen. Recognize the reported concern first, then clarify the surface and arrange the check. An acknowledgement does not require guessing a technical cause or admitting a fault that has not been established.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Acknowledge the concern
Thank you for pointing out the streaks.
Recognize the inconvenience
I am sorry the result is not as expected.
Identify the panel
Is it the interior glass beside office 8?
Ask for a precise indication
Could you point out the affected area?
Avoid dismissing the report
The earlier cleaning does not answer your concern about the current result.
Separate the cause
I do not know what caused the marks yet.
Involve the lead
I will ask Rosa to arrange a check.
Explain the next stage
The check will establish what appropriate rework is needed.
Avoid a guessed remedy
I will not choose a method before the relevant assessment.
Keep status accurate
Rework has not been completed yet.
Check the timing
Let me check when the team can do that.
Preserve the location
Interior panel beside office 8, not the exterior window.
Acknowledge availability
Thank you for being available to show us the panel.
Promise an update
I will pass on the checked next step.
Separate done and verified
A completion report still needs the required verification.
Close the response
I have the concern and location; Rosa will receive the follow-up request.
6 language and precision notes
Reported
This attributes the observation accurately when the worker has not yet checked it.
Earlier today
A time reference does not establish the present quality of the result.
Beside
Keep the location relation precise, especially around numbered offices.
Arrange versus complete
Arranging rework does not mean the corrective work has happened.
Yet
The cause is not known yet; do not fill the gap with blame.
Sorry
An apology for inconvenience need not invent a diagnosis or technical admission.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which opening is most useful?

Answer and explanation

B. Thank you for pointing it out; could you show me the interior panel beside office 8?

The response acknowledges the concern and seeks an exact location without a guessed cause.

2. What does cleaned earlier establish?

Answer and explanation

B. A past cleaning activity, not verified current quality

The earlier activity does not by itself settle the present reported result.

3. Which next step fits the brief?

Answer and explanation

B. Ask Rosa to arrange a check and appropriate rework

Rosa is the named lead who can arrange the required check and response.

4. Which statement falsely closes the issue?

Answer and explanation

C. The panel is now verified clear because the client reported it.

A report supplies a concern, not evidence of completed correction or verification.

Answers and explanations
  1. pointing it out Turn 2. Pointing it out acknowledges the report and invites an accurate location rather than denying the marks.
  2. interior glass Turn 4. Interior glass identifies the inside panel and distinguishes it from the exterior window.
  3. reported result Turn 6. Reported result attributes the streaks to Dana's report while Malik has not yet checked the panel directly.
  4. verification check Turn 8. A verification check examines the current result; the record of earlier cleaning does not settle the new concern.
  5. inspection Turn 10. Inspection is the examination needed to establish the condition and appropriate response before claiming correction.
  6. streaks Turn 12. Streaks is Dana's exact description; scratches would add an unsupported claim of damage.
  7. keep you updated Turn 14. Keep you updated promises communication about checked progress, not an invented finishing time.
  8. rework Turn 16. Rework is corrective work, which must suit the checked surface and condition and has not yet happened.
  9. follow-up request Turn 18. The follow-up request identifies the correct interior panel and Dana's availability to show the marks.
  10. resolved Turn 20. Resolved would claim the concern has been addressed; a report and request alone do not establish that outcome.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Dana: There are still streaks on the glass beside office eight. It was cleaned earlier, but it does not look right.
  2. Malik: Thanks for pointing it out. I am sorry about that. Which panel do you mean?
  3. Dana: The inside panel beside the office door, not the window facing the street.
  4. Malik: The interior glass, beside office eight. I have that. Could you show me where the marks are when we check it?
  5. Dana: Yes, I can show you. I have only noticed streaks; I cannot tell what caused them.
  6. Malik: That helps. I will describe the reported result, not assume a product problem or permanent damage.
  7. Dana: I mentioned it because someone may see the cleaning record and think nothing more needs doing.
  8. Malik: I understand. The earlier work does not replace a verification check of the finish you are telling me about.
  9. Dana: Who should I speak to about getting it checked? I do not want to start again with another person.
  10. Malik: I can pass it to Rosa, our lead. She can arrange an inspection and any appropriate follow-up work.
  11. Dana: Please tell her streaks, not scratches. I do not want the message to sound worse than what I saw.
  12. Malik: Absolutely. I will keep streaks as the description. I have not established a cause.
  13. Dana: Could it be dealt with soon? People will be using the office.
  14. Malik: I will ask about timing. I can keep you updated, but I do not have a confirmed finishing time to give you yet.
  15. Dana: All right. Please let me know what Rosa says rather than telling me it has already been fixed.
  16. Malik: Of course. Any rework will need to suit the actual panel and condition.
  17. Dana: There are several panels along there. Will you mention that I can point out this one?
  18. Malik: Yes. The follow-up request will say interior panel beside office eight, with you available to identify the marks.
  19. Dana: Thank you. That should save us checking the wrong glass.
  20. Malik: I will pass that on to Rosa. We will not mark it resolved simply because the message has been received.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

The panel beside office eight

The glass-quality concern in the case.

  1. Client: There are streaks on the glass you cleaned earlier beside office eight.
  2. Cleaner: Thank you for pointing them out. Is it this interior panel or the outside surface?
  3. Client: This interior panel, especially the upper corner. I'm not referring to the other windows.
  4. Cleaner: I can see the marks you mean. I'll ask our lead to inspect this specific panel.
  5. Client: Can you tell me why they appeared? Was the wrong product used on it?
  6. Cleaner: I don't know the cause. I'll report the visible streaks without guessing about the product.
  7. Client: That's fair. I mainly want the result checked and corrected if it needs rework.
  8. Cleaner: I'll arrange that request through the lead and ask when the panel can be revisited.
  9. Client: Please keep me informed, because the room is free only until the afternoon meeting.
  10. Cleaner: I'll pass on that access limit with the panel location, so the timing can be discussed.
The reported residue is on another desk

A cleaner checks the precise location of a complaint.

  1. Cleaner: You reported residue on a desk. Could you show me which desk you mean?
  2. Office occupant: The one beside the printer, not the reception desk your colleague looked at.
  3. Cleaner: Thank you. That distinction matters because the two desks have different assignment entries.
  4. Office occupant: I'm not saying the whole office was missed. This is the specific surface I'm concerned about.
  5. Cleaner: I'll report this desk and the visible residue, rather than widen the complaint to the entire office.
  6. Office occupant: Can you deal with it while the papers stay spread across the work surface?
  7. Cleaner: I need the agreed access arrangements. Could the owner clear the affected area first?
  8. Office occupant: I'll ask them now. Please don't move or read the papers to find the mark.
  9. Cleaner: I won't. Once access is available, the lead can confirm the appropriate corrective task.
  10. Office occupant: Good. I'll tell you when the surface is clear so that inspection can take place.
Checking the correction with the occupant

A cleaner returns after the assigned rework has been completed.

  1. Cleaner: The lead arranged rework on the marked panel. Could you check the area you identified earlier?
  2. Occupant: Yes, this is the corner I meant. The streaks I reported are no longer visible.
  3. Cleaner: Thank you. Does that resolve your original concern about this panel's appearance?
  4. Occupant: It does. I also noticed a separate mark on the door handle, though.
  5. Cleaner: I'll treat that as a new specific concern, not assume the panel correction failed.
  6. Occupant: That's right. The glass issue is resolved; the handle wasn't part of what I showed you.
  7. Cleaner: Could you point to the handle and describe the visible mark for the next request?
  8. Occupant: It's the meeting-room handle, with a small sticky-looking patch near its base.
  9. Cleaner: I'll report that observation to the lead without claiming what the material is.
  10. Occupant: Thank you. Please close the glass concern and keep the handle request separate.

  2. Repeat Malik's acknowledgement and specific request to Rosa.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Lesson 07: Responding to a quality concern
Original fictional case: Client Dana reports streaks on the interior glass panel beside office 8, which was cleaned earlier today. Cleaner Malik has not yet checked the reported area with the client. Dana is available to point out the panel. Lead Rosa can arrange a check and appropriate rework through the site process. The cause is unknown, and no rework or final inspection is complete. Malik needs to acknowledge the concern, locate it precisely, and explain the next step without denying the report, blaming someone, or promising a result and time that have not been verified.
Language workshop: Streaks beside office 8
Communication goal: Acknowledge a reported result, identify the exact surface, and arrange a check without inventing the cause or outcome.
Language guidance: Streaks are the reported result. They do not prove the wrong product, permanent damage, or who caused the problem. Acknowledge the report and identify the panel before discussing what the actual check establishes.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): Thank you for pointing out the streaks. / I am sorry the result is not as expected. / Is it the interior glass beside office 8? / Could you point out the affected area? / The earlier cleaning does not answer your concern about the current result. / I do not know what caused the marks yet. / I will ask Rosa to arrange a check. / The check will establish what appropriate rework is needed. / I will not choose a method before the relevant assessment. / Rework has not been completed yet. / Let me check when the team can do that. / Interior panel beside office 8, not the exterior window. / Thank you for being available to show us the panel. / I will pass on the checked next step. / A completion report still needs the required verification. / I have the concern and location; Rosa will receive the follow-up request.
Vocabulary: quality concern: Report that a result may not meet the required standard. / streak: Visible line or mark remaining across a surface. / smear: Spread or blurred mark on a surface. / residue: Material remaining after an activity or substance has been removed. / haze: Cloudy appearance that reduces surface clarity. / scratch: Mark or damage caused by abrasion or contact. / interior glass: Glass surface on the inside of a building or assembly. / glass panel: Defined sheet or section of glass. / partition: Structure dividing areas within a space. / frame: Surrounding support or border of a panel. / edge: Boundary of a surface or object. / finish: Visible or specified final condition of a surface. / inspection: Examination against the relevant requirements. / rework: Additional work to correct a result that does not meet requirements. / verification check: Check that the specified result has actually been achieved. / reported result: Outcome described by the person raising the concern. / fluorescent marker: Tracer used to check whether a marked spot was physically cleaned; removal does not prove disinfection. / cause analysis: Investigation of why a result occurred. / service standard: Required level or condition of the provided service. / client feedback: Information from the client about the service received. / follow-up arrangement: Agreed method or responsibility for the next action. / ATP: Adenosine triphosphate; a cleaning-test signal for organic material, not a direct count of specific pathogens. / resolution: Established outcome that addresses the issue through the actual process. / update commitment: Promise to provide information about progress, not a guaranteed result.
Speaking task: Read the complaint exchange, retaining streaks rather than substituting scratches. Repeat Malik's acknowledgement and specific request to Rosa. Complete the office 12 transfer without inventing a remedy, cause, or completed result.
Partner: Dana: Client contact / Malik: Cleaner
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: A client reports smears on the interior panel beside office 12. Lead Ben can arrange a check. The cause is unknown, and no rework has been completed.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Dana: There are several panels along there. Will you mention that I can point out this one?
Malik: Yes. The follow-up request will say interior panel beside office eight, with you available to identify the marks.
Dana: Thank you. That should save us checking the wrong glass.
Malik: I will pass that on to Rosa. We will not mark it resolved simply because the message has been received.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

08Giving a clear end-of-shift handoverThree rooms, different stages+

Your goal

Report each room status separately, assign the unfinished steps, and avoid confusing cleaning with inspection or release.

This lesson in the book, page 67 ↗

A Read the situation

Three rooms, different stages

At the end of the shift, attendant Tessa hands over rooms 301, 302, and 303 to incoming attendant Arun. Rooms 301 and 302 have been cleaned. Room 302 still awaits inspection by supervisor Rosa. Room 303 was inaccessible and has not been cleaned. Arun can accept the access follow-up for 303; Rosa owns the inspection of 302. The handover does not establish room-release decisions or an inspection result for 301. No new room entry, completed inspection, or completed cleaning of 303 occurs during this conversation.

Tessa: Outgoing room attendant / Arun: Incoming room attendant

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. Which rooms have been cleaned?

Answer and explanation

A. 301 and 302

The brief establishes cleaning of 301 and 302, but not of inaccessible 303.

2. What remains for 302?

Answer and explanation

D. Rosa's inspection

Room 302 is cleaned and specifically awaits the supervisor's inspection.

3. What is the status of 303?

Answer and explanation

A. Inaccessible and not cleaned; access follow-up can pass to Arun

Inaccessibility prevented cleaning, and the handover assigns follow-up rather than changing that fact.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Cleaned is not a synonym for inspected or released. Room 302 illustrates the difference: cleaning is complete, but inspection is pending. Room 303 has not been cleaned at all because access was unavailable.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
room status
Current recorded state of a guest room.report room status
cleaned
Cleaning task completed, distinct from later inspection or release.mark the room cleaned
awaiting inspection
Cleaning completed but the required inspection not yet performed.retain awaiting-inspection status
inaccessible
Not available for authorized entry or work at the relevant time.report an inaccessible room
not cleaned
Cleaning has not been carried out.preserve not-cleaned status
room release
Authorization making a room available for the intended use.verify room release
inspection owner
Person responsible for the pending inspection.name the inspection owner
access follow-up
Task of checking and resolving access through the actual procedure.accept an access follow-up
room-status board
Shared display or record of room stages.update the room-status board
handover log
Record of transferred work and current status.maintain the handover log
outgoing shift
Team or work period passing responsibility onward.brief the outgoing shift
incoming shift
Team or work period taking over responsibility.brief the incoming shift
outstanding task
Required work that remains unfinished.identify an outstanding task
task acceptance
Agreement to take responsibility for a task.confirm task acceptance
completion evidence
Information establishing that work actually occurred.require completion evidence
status transition
Change from one recorded stage to another after the relevant event.verify a status transition
inspection result
Actual finding or outcome of the room check.record the inspection result
release authority
Person or process entitled to authorize the next use.confirm release authority
access restriction
Condition limiting entry or work in an area.preserve an access restriction
room identifier
Number or other detail distinguishing the room.repeat the room identifier
follow-up priority
Relative order or urgency assigned to unfinished tasks.clarify follow-up priority
exception report
Record of a task or condition outside the expected completed state.submit an exception report
carryover work
Unfinished work transferred to the next shift.track carryover work
separate status
Distinct recorded state for each room or task.preserve separate statuses

The handover supplies only a cleaned status for room 301. Do not invent either a passed inspection or a failed one. A room-release decision must come from the actual authorized process, not from the absence of a problem in a short report.

C Notice the language

Precision survives a shift change

A short list can carry more useful information than a general all done message. Repeat each room number with its actual stage and the person responsible for the next step. Receiving the list should make ownership clear without upgrading an unfinished status.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Start the room list
I have separate updates for 301, 302, and 303.
State the first fact
Room 301 has been cleaned.
Avoid an invented result
I have no inspection result for 301 to add.
State the second stage
Room 302 is cleaned and awaiting inspection.
Name the inspection owner
Rosa owns the inspection for 302.
State the access issue
Room 303 was inaccessible.
Preserve the consequence
Room 303 has not been cleaned.
Assign follow-up
Can you take the access follow-up for 303?
Accept without overclaiming
I accept the follow-up; access is not resolved yet.
Keep entry separate
The actual access procedure still applies.
Avoid automatic release
This handover does not authorize room release.
Repeat the unfinished work
Inspection for 302 and access follow-up for 303 remain open.
Update by event
Change the status only when the relevant work or decision occurs.
Keep room numbers attached
Please record each stage against the correct room.
Confirm the record
The handover log should match these separate statuses.
Close with ownership
Rosa will inspect 302; you will check access to 303.
6 language and precision notes
Cleaned versus ready
Ready may imply more than cleaning; name the actual completed stage.
Awaiting
Awaiting inspection explicitly identifies a pending next step.
Was inaccessible
This explains the earlier condition but does not prove present access has changed.
Accepted versus completed
A receiving worker can accept responsibility while work remains unfinished.
No result supplied
Missing information is not evidence of either passing or failing an inspection.
Separate entries
Keep each room number attached to its own state and next owner.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which summary is accurate?

Answer and explanation

B. 301 cleaned; 302 cleaned, inspection pending; 303 inaccessible and not cleaned.

The summary preserves the distinct completed and unfinished stages for each room.

2. What does Arun accept?

Answer and explanation

C. Access follow-up for 303

Arun accepts responsibility for checking access, not completed cleaning or room-release authority.

3. What can be said about 301's inspection?

Answer and explanation

A. No result is supplied in this handover.

The brief supplies cleaned status only and does not establish any inspection outcome.

4. When should a status change?

Answer and explanation

D. After the relevant actual work or authorized decision

Status records should follow real events and permissions rather than expectations or shift changes.

Check explanations in the book, page 99 ↗

E Conversations

Three rooms, different stages

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Tessa: Outgoing room attendant / Arun: Incoming room attendant

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • cleaned
  • not cleaned
  • awaiting inspection
  • handover log
  • Room release
  • access follow-up
  • separate status
  • task acceptance
  • status transition
  • inspection owner
  1. Tessa
    Can I run through rooms three oh one, three oh two, and three oh three before I leave?
  2. Arun
    I am ready to take the 1 details. Give me each room number with what has actually happened and what remains for the next person.
  3. Tessa
    Room three hundred one has been cleaned. I have no inspection result or room-release decision to add to that statement.
  4. Arun
    I will record 2 for 301 without inventing another stage. The absence of an inspection result in this message does not mean I can assume one.
  5. Tessa
    Room three hundred two has also been cleaned, but it still awaits Rosa's inspection. That unfinished step needs to remain visible.
  6. Arun
    So 302 is 3, with cleaning complete. I will keep that distinction on the room-status record instead of shortening it to ready.
  7. Tessa
    Rosa is still doing the inspection for three oh two. I only need you to carry that information forward.
  8. Arun
    Rosa is the 4. I can carry the status forward, but I should not report that she has already completed the check.
  9. Tessa
    Room three hundred three was inaccessible, so it has not been cleaned. Can you take the access follow-up through the actual procedure?
  10. Arun
    Yes, I accept the 5 for 303. That means I will address the access question through the proper route, not enter just because the shift has changed.
  11. Tessa
    Thanks. Three oh three stays not cleaned until the work is actually done.
  12. Arun
    I will preserve the 6 status. A planned follow-up is not completion evidence, and I will not upgrade the room based on an intention.
  13. Tessa
    Good. If someone asks whether all three rooms can be released, this handover alone does not supply that decision for any of them.
  14. Arun
    Understood. 7 belongs to the actual authorized process. I will not substitute our conversation for the required inspection or release decision.
  15. Tessa
    Can you repeat who has the inspection and who has the access follow-up?
  16. Arun
    Rosa has the inspection for 302; I have the access follow-up for 303. That is my 8, not a report that either outstanding task is finished.
  17. Tessa
    That is accurate. Room 301 is reported cleaned only. Please do not borrow 302's inspection status and apply it to 301 without information.
  18. Arun
    I will keep a 9 for each room. Shared numbering does not make the stages identical, and an unknown result should remain unknown.
  19. Tessa
    That matches my list. Please add the real outcomes to the right room as the checks happen.
  20. Arun
    Yes. Each 10 will follow the relevant event. For now: 301 cleaned, 302 cleaned and awaiting Rosa's inspection, 303 inaccessible and not cleaned, with access follow-up assigned to me.

Answers and explanations
  1. handover log Turn 2. Handover log records the separate room states and next tasks, rather than treating the end of a shift as completion.
  2. cleaned Turn 4. Cleaned is the established stage for room 301; no inspection result or release decision should be added.
  3. awaiting inspection Turn 6. Awaiting inspection preserves the pending check after cleaning, distinguishing room 302 from an inspected or released room.
  4. inspection owner Turn 8. Inspection owner identifies Rosa's responsibility for room 302; receiving a handover does not automatically transfer that task.
  5. access follow-up Turn 10. Access follow-up is the task Arun accepts for room 303; it is not authorization for immediate entry or proof of cleaning.
  6. not cleaned Turn 12. Not cleaned is room 303's actual cleaning state; accepting an access task does not change it.
  7. Room release Turn 14. Room release is a separate authorized decision, which cannot be inferred from cleaning, task acceptance, or this handover alone.
  8. task acceptance Turn 16. Task acceptance confirms Arun's responsibility for the access follow-up while leaving the inspection with Rosa and both tasks unfinished.
  9. separate status Turn 18. Separate status maintains a distinct evidence-based entry for each room instead of copying another room's stage or assuming all are alike.
  10. status transition Turn 20. Status transition should follow the actual task result or authorized decision, not the arrival of a new shift or a repeated promise.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Tessa: Can I run through rooms three oh one, three oh two, and three oh three before I leave?
  2. Arun: I am ready to take the handover log details. Give me each room number with what has actually happened and what remains for the next person.
  3. Tessa: Room three hundred one has been cleaned. I have no inspection result or room-release decision to add to that statement.
  4. Arun: I will record cleaned for 301 without inventing another stage. The absence of an inspection result in this message does not mean I can assume one.
  5. Tessa: Room three hundred two has also been cleaned, but it still awaits Rosa's inspection. That unfinished step needs to remain visible.
  6. Arun: So 302 is awaiting inspection, with cleaning complete. I will keep that distinction on the room-status record instead of shortening it to ready.
  7. Tessa: Rosa is still doing the inspection for three oh two. I only need you to carry that information forward.
  8. Arun: Rosa is the inspection owner. I can carry the status forward, but I should not report that she has already completed the check.
  9. Tessa: Room three hundred three was inaccessible, so it has not been cleaned. Can you take the access follow-up through the actual procedure?
  10. Arun: Yes, I accept the access follow-up for 303. That means I will address the access question through the proper route, not enter just because the shift has changed.
  11. Tessa: Thanks. Three oh three stays not cleaned until the work is actually done.
  12. Arun: I will preserve the not cleaned status. A planned follow-up is not completion evidence, and I will not upgrade the room based on an intention.
  13. Tessa: Good. If someone asks whether all three rooms can be released, this handover alone does not supply that decision for any of them.
  14. Arun: Understood. Room release belongs to the actual authorized process. I will not substitute our conversation for the required inspection or release decision.
  15. Tessa: Can you repeat who has the inspection and who has the access follow-up?
  16. Arun: Rosa has the inspection for 302; I have the access follow-up for 303. That is my task acceptance, not a report that either outstanding task is finished.
  17. Tessa: That is accurate. Room 301 is reported cleaned only. Please do not borrow 302's inspection status and apply it to 301 without information.
  18. Arun: I will keep a separate status for each room. Shared numbering does not make the stages identical, and an unknown result should remain unknown.
  19. Tessa: That matches my list. Please add the real outcomes to the right room as the checks happen.
  20. Arun: Yes. Each status transition will follow the relevant event. For now: 301 cleaned, 302 cleaned and awaiting Rosa's inspection, 303 inaccessible and not cleaned, with access follow-up assigned to me.
Dialogue in the book, page 73 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Three rooms, three statuses

The end-of-shift case on the third floor.

  1. Outgoing attendant: Room three-oh-one is cleaned. Three-oh-two is cleaned too, but it still awaits the supervisor's inspection.
  2. Incoming attendant: What about three-oh-three? Its room status doesn't show a completed cleaning task.
  3. Outgoing attendant: It was inaccessible, so cleaning hasn't started. Can you take its access follow-up?
  4. Incoming attendant: Yes, I'll check access for three-oh-three and update its status once I know more.
  5. Outgoing attendant: Thank you. I'll confirm the inspection request for three-oh-two with the supervisor before leaving.
  6. Incoming attendant: Then I'm not being asked to release three-oh-two or describe its inspection as complete?
  7. Outgoing attendant: Correct. The supervisor's sign-off is separate. Please focus on checking access to three-oh-three.
  8. Incoming attendant: I'll repeat the status: three-oh-one cleaned; three-oh-two awaiting inspection; three-oh-three uncleaned because access was unavailable.
  9. Outgoing attendant: That's the current picture. Please report any change rather than carry yesterday's assumptions forward.
  10. Incoming attendant: I will. I'll start by contacting the person responsible for access to three-oh-three.
A completed area has one limitation

A commercial cleaner gives a precise end-of-shift report.

  1. Cleaner: The west office floor is complete except the space beneath the blocked storage shelf.
  2. Incoming colleague: Was that space inaccessible throughout the visit, or did you leave it for another reason?
  3. Cleaner: It was blocked by stored boxes. I wasn't assigned to move them.
  4. Incoming colleague: Then I'll keep that limitation in the completion record rather than mark every area finished.
  5. Cleaner: The office contact has agreed to arrange clearance before tomorrow's visit, but it hasn't happened yet.
  6. Incoming colleague: I'll check whether the boxes are moved before reporting that the remaining area can be reached.
  7. Cleaner: Everything else on that floor's list is complete, including bins and the shared table surfaces.
  8. Incoming colleague: Thanks. Is the blocked area the only outstanding task I'm inheriting for that office?
  9. Cleaner: Yes. The separate maintenance request for the corridor light belongs to the building team.
  10. Incoming colleague: I'll keep the floor limitation and the maintenance request separate in my handover update.
The receiver cannot take the task

An outgoing cleaner finds that the intended colleague is assigned elsewhere.

  1. Outgoing cleaner: Could you take the final washroom supply check? It hasn't been completed on my shift.
  2. Colleague: I can't accept it yet; I'm assigned to another building for the rest of tonight.
  3. Outgoing cleaner: Thanks for saying so. I won't mark it handed over just because we've discussed it.
  4. Colleague: Please ask the supervisor to reassign it. They know who is staying at this site.
  5. Outgoing cleaner: I'll keep it listed as outstanding and contact them before I finish my report.
  6. Colleague: Tell them the exact washroom and which check is missing so they can allocate it.
  7. Outgoing cleaner: It's washroom C's paper-towel replenishment check, not a new full-room cleaning request.
  8. Colleague: That distinction should help them choose the appropriate worker and avoid duplicating completed cleaning.
  9. Outgoing cleaner: The supervisor has accepted the reassignment question. I'll record that, with the check still unfinished.
  10. Colleague: Good. Someone has the allocation follow-up now, but the supply check itself remains outstanding.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

Three rooms, different stages
  1. Read the handover, pausing between each room number and status.
  2. Repeat Rosa's inspection task separately from Arun's access task.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Hand over another room sequence

Room 410 is cleaned. Room 411 is cleaned and awaits inspection by Ben. Room 412 was inaccessible and is not cleaned. Incoming attendant Jo accepts the access follow-up for 412.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Outgoing: "The room awaiting inspection is ___."

Answer and explanation

D. 411

Room 411 is cleaned but still requires its identified inspection.

2. Incoming: "The inspection owner is ___."

Answer and explanation

B. Ben

Ben is named as responsible for the pending room inspection.

3. Outgoing: "The inaccessible, uncleaned room is ___."

Answer and explanation

B. 412

Room 412 has not been cleaned because access was unavailable.

4. Incoming: "The access follow-up has been accepted by ___."

Answer and explanation

B. Jo

Jo accepts the follow-up, which does not establish completed access or cleaning.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

REFERENCE
Course: Housekeeping and Commercial Cleaning English
Professional audience: Housekeeping attendants, commercial cleaners, custodial staff, and cleaning team members
Scope: All sites, rooms, quantities, guests, and incidents are fictional. This book teaches workplace English, not chemical handling, equipment operation, hazard control, legal advice, or specialist infection prevention. Follow actual training, product labels and safety data sheets, site access rules, privacy and found-property procedures, and emergency arrangements. No example authorizes unsafe access, use of an unidentified product, or release of an unchecked area.
Lesson 08: Giving a clear end-of-shift handover
Original fictional case: At the end of the shift, attendant Tessa hands over rooms 301, 302, and 303 to incoming attendant Arun. Rooms 301 and 302 have been cleaned. Room 302 still awaits inspection by supervisor Rosa. Room 303 was inaccessible and has not been cleaned. Arun can accept the access follow-up for 303; Rosa owns the inspection of 302. The handover does not establish room-release decisions or an inspection result for 301. No new room entry, completed inspection, or completed cleaning of 303 occurs during this conversation.
Language workshop: Three rooms, different stages
Communication goal: Report each room status separately, assign the unfinished steps, and avoid confusing cleaning with inspection or release.
Language guidance: Cleaned is not a synonym for inspected or released. Room 302 illustrates the difference: cleaning is complete, but inspection is pending. Room 303 has not been cleaned at all because access was unavailable.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): I have separate updates for 301, 302, and 303. / Room 301 has been cleaned. / I have no inspection result for 301 to add. / Room 302 is cleaned and awaiting inspection. / Rosa owns the inspection for 302. / Room 303 was inaccessible. / Room 303 has not been cleaned. / Can you take the access follow-up for 303? / I accept the follow-up; access is not resolved yet. / The actual access procedure still applies. / This handover does not authorize room release. / Inspection for 302 and access follow-up for 303 remain open. / Change the status only when the relevant work or decision occurs. / Please record each stage against the correct room. / The handover log should match these separate statuses. / Rosa will inspect 302; you will check access to 303.
Vocabulary: room status: Current recorded state of a guest room. / cleaned: Cleaning task completed, distinct from later inspection or release. / awaiting inspection: Cleaning completed but the required inspection not yet performed. / inaccessible: Not available for authorized entry or work at the relevant time. / not cleaned: Cleaning has not been carried out. / room release: Authorization making a room available for the intended use. / inspection owner: Person responsible for the pending inspection. / access follow-up: Task of checking and resolving access through the actual procedure. / room-status board: Shared display or record of room stages. / handover log: Record of transferred work and current status. / outgoing shift: Team or work period passing responsibility onward. / incoming shift: Team or work period taking over responsibility. / outstanding task: Required work that remains unfinished. / task acceptance: Agreement to take responsibility for a task. / completion evidence: Information establishing that work actually occurred. / status transition: Change from one recorded stage to another after the relevant event. / inspection result: Actual finding or outcome of the room check. / release authority: Person or process entitled to authorize the next use. / access restriction: Condition limiting entry or work in an area. / room identifier: Number or other detail distinguishing the room. / follow-up priority: Relative order or urgency assigned to unfinished tasks. / exception report: Record of a task or condition outside the expected completed state. / carryover work: Unfinished work transferred to the next shift. / separate status: Distinct recorded state for each room or task.
Speaking task: Read the handover, pausing between each room number and status. Repeat Rosa's inspection task separately from Arun's access task. Complete the new room sequence and attach each owner to the right room.
Partner: Tessa: Outgoing room attendant / Arun: Incoming room attendant
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Room 410 is cleaned. Room 411 is cleaned and awaits inspection by Ben. Room 412 was inaccessible and is not cleaned. Incoming attendant Jo accepts the access follow-up for 412.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Tessa: That is accurate. Room 301 is reported cleaned only. Please do not borrow 302's inspection status and apply it to 301 without information.
Arun: I will keep a separate status for each room. Shared numbering does not make the stages identical, and an unknown result should remain unknown.
Tessa: That matches my list. Please add the real outcomes to the right room as the checks happen.
Arun: Yes. Each status transition will follow the relevant event. For now: 301 cleaned, 302 cleaned and awaiting Rosa's inspection, 303 inaccessible and not cleaned, with access follow-up assigned to me.
END REFERENCE
Practice grammar in context — complete prompt

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

More situations, different skills

Three additional workplace scenarios

The clock is not the whole check

Read a product-use requirement accurately and explain why elapsed time alone does not establish compliance.

Fictional record review, not chemical-use instructions: the approved product directions for the specified hard, nonporous surface require five minutes continuously visibly wet. The recorded application was 07:42; the surface was observed wet until it dried at 07:46. At 07:47, worker Farah asks whether the requirement was met. No correction or release is recorded.

Farah: Cleaner reviewing the record / Ben: Cleaning supervisor

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • directions for use
  • application record
  • surface compatibility
  • five minutes
  • visibly wet
  • 07:47
  1. Farah
    The timer reached five minutes at seven forty-seven, but the surface had dried a minute before that. Can I mark the contact-time requirement complete?
  2. Ben
    No. Check the 1, not only the timer. What exact condition is attached to those five minutes?
  3. Farah
    The specified hard, nonporous surface has to stay visibly wet continuously. The recorded start was seven forty-two, and it dried at seven forty-six.
  4. Ben
    Then the stated 2 were not achieved in that record. Four minutes wet followed by one dry minute is not the same condition.
  5. Farah
    If it had stayed wet continuously from seven forty-two, the five-minute point would have been seven forty-seven.
  6. Ben
    Yes, 3 is the arithmetic endpoint. It does not override what actually happened to the surface before then.
  7. Farah
    I wrote the drying time in my notes. I should retain that rather than change the entry to match what the timer was meant to show.
  8. Ben
    Exactly. The 4 needs the observed facts. We can then arrange the required correction under the actual product directions and site procedure.
  9. Farah
    Would a photograph at the start be enough to prove that the surface stayed wet throughout?
  10. Ben
    No. It shows that moment only. The requirement is 5 throughout the specified period, not just for one photograph.
  11. Farah
    Someone suggested using the same timing on the upholstered chair beside it. Is that covered by the hard-surface instruction?
  12. Ben
    Not from this information. Check 6 and the approved use for that material. The hard-surface instruction is not permission to treat upholstery the same way.
  13. Farah
    The product's safety data sheet is available too. Should I take a remembered time from that instead of this use instruction?
  14. Ben
    Use the current applicable directions for the exact product and task, with the matching hazard information and training. Do not replace them with a remembered number.
  15. Farah
    And I should not improvise a stronger concentration or combine products to try to shorten the time.
  16. Ben
    Correct. This record review does not authorize a new mixture or application method. Any correction must follow the real instructions and workplace controls.
  17. Farah
    I will report application at seven forty-two, drying at seven forty-six, and the five-minute wet condition not met. No correction has yet been recorded.
  18. Ben
    That is accurate. The issue is the unmet condition, not a claim that the timer malfunctioned or that the surface was never cleaned.
  19. Farah
    Once the authorized correction is performed, I will record it separately with its actual observations rather than erase the earlier result.
  20. Ben
    Good. Keep the original record and follow-up linked. A planned correction, an elapsed five minutes, and an approved return to use are separate facts.

Answers and explanations
  1. directions for use Turn 2. Directions for use specify the relevant surface and application conditions; the timer alone does not establish that they were met.
  2. five minutes Turn 4. The fictional directions require five continuous wet minutes; four wet minutes plus one dry minute do not satisfy that requirement.
  3. 07:47 Turn 6. 07:42 plus five minutes is 07:47, but that clock time would meet the duration only if the required wet condition had continued.
  4. application record Turn 8. The application record must retain the actual start and drying times rather than being edited into an unsupported successful result.
  5. visibly wet Turn 10. Visibly wet describes the required surface condition throughout the contact period, not merely at the start or the final photograph.
  6. surface compatibility Turn 12. Surface compatibility and approved use must be established for the actual material; hard-nonporous directions do not automatically cover upholstery.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Farah: The timer reached five minutes at seven forty-seven, but the surface had dried a minute before that. Can I mark the contact-time requirement complete?
  2. Ben: No. Check the directions for use, not only the timer. What exact condition is attached to those five minutes?
  3. Farah: The specified hard, nonporous surface has to stay visibly wet continuously. The recorded start was seven forty-two, and it dried at seven forty-six.
  4. Ben: Then the stated five minutes were not achieved in that record. Four minutes wet followed by one dry minute is not the same condition.
  5. Farah: If it had stayed wet continuously from seven forty-two, the five-minute point would have been seven forty-seven.
  6. Ben: Yes, 07:47 is the arithmetic endpoint. It does not override what actually happened to the surface before then.
  7. Farah: I wrote the drying time in my notes. I should retain that rather than change the entry to match what the timer was meant to show.
  8. Ben: Exactly. The application record needs the observed facts. We can then arrange the required correction under the actual product directions and site procedure.
  9. Farah: Would a photograph at the start be enough to prove that the surface stayed wet throughout?
  10. Ben: No. It shows that moment only. The requirement is visibly wet throughout the specified period, not just for one photograph.
  11. Farah: Someone suggested using the same timing on the upholstered chair beside it. Is that covered by the hard-surface instruction?
  12. Ben: Not from this information. Check surface compatibility and the approved use for that material. The hard-surface instruction is not permission to treat upholstery the same way.
  13. Farah: The product's safety data sheet is available too. Should I take a remembered time from that instead of this use instruction?
  14. Ben: Use the current applicable directions for the exact product and task, with the matching hazard information and training. Do not replace them with a remembered number.
  15. Farah: And I should not improvise a stronger concentration or combine products to try to shorten the time.
  16. Ben: Correct. This record review does not authorize a new mixture or application method. Any correction must follow the real instructions and workplace controls.
  17. Farah: I will report application at seven forty-two, drying at seven forty-six, and the five-minute wet condition not met. No correction has yet been recorded.
  18. Ben: That is accurate. The issue is the unmet condition, not a claim that the timer malfunctioned or that the surface was never cleaned.
  19. Farah: Once the authorized correction is performed, I will record it separately with its actual observations rather than erase the earlier result.
  20. Ben: Good. Keep the original record and follow-up linked. A planned correction, an elapsed five minutes, and an approved return to use are separate facts.
Retain the wet-condition failure

Fictional directions require three continuous wet minutes on the specified surface. Application was 14:10; it remained wet until drying at 14:12. No later correction is recorded. Calculate time and report status only; do not invent treatment instructions.

Answer and explanation

D. three

The supplied directions require three minutes, not the two minutes actually observed wet.

2. Supervisor: With continuous wetness, the arithmetic endpoint would be ___ .

Answer and explanation
